# Campaign of 2026-09-30, campaign tier, present arm, sufficiency

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:1c21e55eb09aca89b3d736ac79f8accfc64ddd7853bace5fb5e577a82d794bb9
lock: sha256:4b95fd419a3824634b9644c91b13356ba9b71567f813445913c430e3aa12c13a
selection: sha256:1d93b514529314b74499196391c68b4b3d1de26853dd9d5ba7f3b455f54ed91d
read_set: sha256:14520e15ae3a34b9bede8c6702c8b2ab7046cf513c2ba5e03f9fc4628cb2cc31
seed: 0
harness: 0.5.0
tier: campaign
arm: present
at: 2026-09-30
cost_cents: 6557
```

**This is the full re-run of the counterfactual campaign that [#1384](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1384) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it on 2026-09-30 (UTC), at the 30 repetitions per probe that the tier declares, after the changes to the recorder, the grader, the read set and the seal had merged (#1394, #1396, #1414 and #1413) and after #1448 had merged. The owner ruled on #1384 on 2026-09-30 that the batch runs the campaign tier's sufficiency, navigability and discovery selections and the documentation tier's absent arm, under a cap of 33000 cents for the whole batch.

**One batch, one tree.** Every session of the seven lines of the batch ran in a fresh copy of one `git archive` of commit `109abba7`, an empty pin commit on `eb10cd86`, with the engine that commit builds. The sessions ran in one order that seed 980 shuffled, on `claude-sonnet-5` with a cap of 80 turns. The first 186 sessions ran four at a time. At about 08:25 UTC the owner raised the cap of the batch to 40000 cents and the workers to eight, so the driver was stopped and started once more on the same pin and the same order. The four sessions that were in flight at the stop were drawn again from the start, and no recorded session ran twice. The cap stopped 1 of the 119 sessions of this line. Each workspace lost the instrument, every record under `docs/` that names a probe of the batch, and every file outside `docs/` that names one and is not a declared fold. Each absent workspace also lost the ablation of its tier. The two arms of each pair read one pin, and the pin is not the commit that this transcript merges on, so the `tree` digest above names the corpus the sessions read and not the corpus a reader has. The `seed` in the identity above is the plan's seed, and the shuffle seed is not a member of the identity.

**The oracle.** A `patched` artifact of either arm was checked in a copy of the present tree with the artifact written into it (`probe-transform.sh --oracle-tree`), so one oracle graded both arms. The recorder was given the arm's tree as `--baseline`, so a file that a session wrote through `Bash` is in `produced`.

**This line recorded 119 of its 120 planned sessions.** The session that did not record is repetition 12 of the probe on an unmeasured claim. The session wrote an obligation record and did not leave it at the path it named, so `probe-transform.sh` could not copy the artifact into the oracle tree, and the recorder exited 5. The session was not drawn again, because a second pass draws only the sessions that failed. The denominator of every rate on this line is the 119 sessions above.

**It succeeds `HW-RUN-campaign-of-2026-09-28-campaign-tier-present-arm-sufficiency`.** The owner retired that recording on 2026-09-29, because #1294 moved the read set of its selection. Both name the selection `sha256:1d93b514529314b74499196391c68b4b3d1de26853dd9d5ba7f3b455f54ed91d`, on the same model and served version. This transcript declares no `supersedes` edge to it. The edge sets its target to `superseded`, the retired recording stands at `deprecated`, and the lifecycle regime `standard` admits no movement out of `deprecated`. So the edge would name a state its target cannot reach, and this paragraph records the succession in its place.

**What the seal cannot find.** The seal and its guard find an answer only by a name, and the **Limit** paragraph of the `folds:` comment in `.headwater/probe.yml` states that bound. In the sealed present tree, 69 of the 529 entries of `.headwater/nav.yml` name a file that the tree does not hold. Each of the 69 is on one of the three shelves of the instrument (`docs/probes/`, `docs/probe-runs/` and `docs/probe-results/`), which `ablate.sh --present` removes and the seal does not edit out of that fold. So a present-arm session can read the title of each probe in `nav.yml` and cannot open the file. No absent arm holds `nav.yml`. This is a difference between the arms that nothing controls.

**What the workspace did not hold in.** A session ran with its workspace as the working directory, and nothing stopped it from reading outside it. 9 of the 119 sessions of this line made a call that names a path outside the workspace, in the checkout of this repository or in the directory that held the batch, and 0 of them named the checkout. That directory held the trees and the task files of the batch and the trees of the pilot of 2026-09-29. A session that read a file there could read a document the seal had removed. The comment on #1384 that reports this batch gives each rate with and without these sessions.

      argument: "{\"command\":\"HW=/home/james/.claude/jobs/1338ac1e/tmp/issue-1384/full/campaign/ws/L1-campaign-present-p4-r20\\nFILE=\\\"$HW/docs/obligations/0227-spec-5-claims-that-claude-md-costs-context-on-every-session-and-no-reading-states-how-much.md\\\"\\ncat > \\\"$FILE\\\" <<'HWEOF'\\n---\\nid: HW-OBL-0227\\nstatus: current\\nstatus_since: 2026-09-30\\nsummary: \\\"The always-loaded instruction surface names its own cost in prose, and this corpus has never measured it in tokens, unlike the millisecond readings spec 5 gives route, check and the MCP server.\\\"\\nlast_verified: 2026-09-30\\ntitle: \\\"Spec 5 claims that CLAUDE.md costs context on every session, and no reading states how much\\\"\\nwaiting_on: measurement\\nprovenance:\\n  warrant: asserted\\n  agency: agent\\n  drafted_by: claude-sonnet-5\\n  activity: draft\\n  evidence_basis: evidenced\\nrelations:\\n  traces_to:\\n    - HW-SPEC-ai-integration\\n---\\n\\n# Spec 5 claims that CLAUDE.md costs context on every session, and no reading states how much\\n\\n## Context\\n\\n[Spec 5](../spec/05-ai-integration.md#how-a-skill-reaches-an-agent-and-what-nothing-does) states that an instruction file a harness always loads \\\"reaches the model before any judgment about relevance\\\" and \\\"costs context on every session, and most sessions touch no document at all.\\\" It names the payers of that cost in this repository: `CLAUDE.md`, `ste-editor`, and the two skills the surrounding paragraph names. The same specification gives a number for every other cost it raises. It reports `headwater check --strict` at 233 ms warm and 587 ms with `--no-cache` over 489 documents, `headwater route` at 35 to 160 ms across two corpus sizes, and one MCP `check` call at about 504 ms, each reading dated, sized to a document count, and reproducible from a named script under `tools/measure/`. The always-loaded instruction surface gets one sentence and no reading at all.\\n\\n## Obligation\\n\\nThis corpus owes a reading of what its own standing instructions cost a session, taken the way spec 5 already takes a reading of route and check: named, dated, and reproducible from a script this repository commits. The count is what a session pays in tokens for `CLAUDE.md`, the skill directives a hook always sends, and any routed pointers a hook prints before the first tool call, ahead of any task narrowing what the session reads next. A reading should separate what a hook adds per prompt from what a harness loads once per session, because the two do not grow with the corpus the same way.\\n\\n## Discharge\\n\\nNo script under `tools/measure/` takes this reading. `tools/measure/mcp-check.sh` and `tools/measure/large-governs.sh` both report CPU time, and neither counts a token. The record stays open until a session runs an instrument that reports the token count of the standing instruction surface at a stated corpus size, the way the two existing scripts report CPU time at a stated document count. It waits on that measurement rather than on a ruling, because no open question decides what the number should be, only what the number is.\\nHWEOF\\necho \\\"wrote $(wc -l < \\\"$FILE\\\") lines\\\"\",\"description\":\"Write the obligation record body via heredoc since Edit is sandboxed away from the shared checkout\"}"

It never measured `CLAUDE.md` itself, the file its own decision test 5 sends every rule to (\\\"Every agent must obey it. It lives in `CLAUDE.md`, and nothing else goes there.\\\").\\n\\n`CLAUDE.md` is 9,097 bytes, 1,313 words and 79 lines, a word count taken by hand and not a token count taken by a tokenizer. The per-dispatch cost HW-PD-0001 names is real and repeats at that multiplicity, and nothing here says whether it is small enough to ignore or large enough to trim.\\n\\n## Obligation\\n\\nThis corpus owes a token count of `CLAUDE.md`, taken with the actual tokenizer each agent family runs on rather than estimated from the word count above. Where more than one tokenizer reads the file, the corpus owes a count per tokenizer, because a word count is not a stand-in for either. It also owes the same count multiplied by a realistic dispatch count for one `next-run` pass, so a reader meets an order of magnitude for a run and not only for one dispatch. Record where the measurement was taken and what it found, in the shape HW-PD-0001 used for `next-run.md`. Nothing here asks for a size ceiling, a gate or a rewrite of `CLAUDE.md`; a measurement that finds the current size already cheap enough is a legitimate answer, and the file's own author never checked.\\n\\n## Discharge\\n\\nA session that runs a real tokenizer over `CLAUDE.md`, for each agent family that reads it, and records the resulting counts and their per-run multiple where a future reader meets them, discharges this. The evaluation shelf is where HW-PD-0001 put the same kind of measurement for `next-run.md`, and a sibling record there or an update to this record's Discharge section both serve. A plain statement that the count was taken and the cost is acceptable, with no follow-up, is a legitimate close. Leaving the claim in `CLAUDE.md`'s own first sentence unmeasured is not.\\nDOCEOF\\necho WROTE; wc -l docs/obligations/0227-*.md\",\"description\":\"Write the obligation record content via a heredoc\"}"

    - tool: "Bash"
      argument: "{\"command\":\"D=/home/james/.claude/jobs/1338ac1e/tmp/issue-1384/full/campaign/ws/L1-campaign-present-p4-r9\\nF=\\\"$D/docs/obligations/0227-nothing-states-how-much-of-a-session-s-budget-the-standing-instructions-of-this-repository-consume-before-any-work-starts.md\\\"\\ncat > \\\"$F\\\" <<'DOCEOF'\\n---\\nid: HW-OBL-0227\\nstatus: current\\nstatus_since: 2026-09-30\\nsummary: \\\"Spec 5 names CLAUDE.md as a cost every session pays before judgment about relevance, and no reading in this corpus prices that cost in tokens or in the context budget spec 6 sizes everything else against.\\\"\\nlast_verified: 2026-09-30\\ntitle: \\\"Nothing states how much of a session's budget the standing instructions of this repository consume before any work starts\\\"\\nwaiting_on: measurement\\nprovenance:\\n  warrant: asserted\\n  agency: agent\\n  drafted_by: claude-sonnet-5\\n  activity: draft\\n  evidence_basis: evidenced\\n---\\n\\n# Nothing states how much of a session's budget the standing instructions of this repository consume before any work starts\\n\\n## Context\\n\\n[Spec 5](../spec/05-ai-integration.md#how-a-skill-reaches-an-agent-and-what-nothing-does) names an instruction file that a harness always loads as a directive rather than a cue. The file reaches the model before any judgment about relevance. The same paragraph states that this repository pays that cost in `CLAUDE.md`, in `ste-editor`, and in two more skills, on every session, whether or not the session opens a document.\\n\\nSpec 5 also states a budget for a different class of always-loaded text. A generated rule obeys a size budget that its kind or projection declares, and the engine meters that budget per file and in total on every request. An over-budget rule is a finding a check reports. `CLAUDE.md` and a skill's own instruction text are not a generated rule, so that metered budget does not cover them.\\n\\n[Spec 6](../spec/06-engine-architecture.md) prices almost every other cost this engine names, in the same shape each time: a count, the corpus size, the date, and the script that took the reading. A warm `headwater check` cost 233 ms of CPU time on 2026-09-30, at 489 typed documents. A `headwater route` call cost 60 ms on 2026-09-28, at 444 checked documents. [HW-OBL-0080](0080-changed-only-is-the-content-addressed-cache-under-another-name.md) carries a reading of the commit hook against its own 200 ms target. No paragraph of this corpus states a comparable count for the standing instructions a session reads before it opens a file.\\n\\n## Obligation\\n\\nThe corpus owes a reading of the cost spec 5 names, taken the way spec 6 takes every other reading: a token or word count, a date, the engine and harness version, and the script that produced it. A claim that a mechanism costs context on every session, with no number beside it, is a claim this corpus would refuse from any other subsystem. `CLAUDE.md`, `ste-editor`, and the two skills spec 5 names beside them are the concrete lines such a count would total first, because spec 5 already names them as the payers.\\n\\nA second question rides with the first and this record does not answer it. Spec 6 prices a run against a budget stated in seconds or milliseconds. Nothing in this corpus states a budget for the tokens a standing instruction may spend before any work starts, so a reading with no budget to compare against states a number and settles nothing beside it.\\n\\n## Discharge\\n\\nNothing here is discharged. A reading would move it: a script under `tools/measure/`, run against the current `CLAUDE.md` and the instruction text of each skill a harness always loads, recorded beside the check and route numbers spec 6 already carries, with the date, the corpus size, and the harness version it was taken under. `headwater capture` and the capture-cost store give this corpus a model for the recording shape, though the count this record asks for is a property of the harness prompt rather than a reading `headwater new` takes.\\n\\nA ruling could discharge this record a second way, by stating that the cost of a standing instruction is a property of the harness and never a fact this corpus prices, the way [spec 7](../spec/07-distribution-and-federation.md) rules that a vendored package root sits outside the corpus root and outside every rule that reads one. No such ruling exists yet. Until a reading or a ruling lands, the gap this record names is silence rather than a measured position.\\nDOCEOF\\necho \\\"wrote $(wc -l < \\\"$F\\\") lines\\\"\",\"description\":\"Write the filled-in obligation document content via Bash heredoc\"}"
